If it goes up by even a single pound, I feel like a complete failure as a person and a teacher.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you don't purge or throw up after bingeing, but you do engage in excessive exercise as a form of punishment, can you tell me more about what you mean by 'forcing myself to exercise until I'm completely lightheaded'?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
After a huge night of eating, I'll skip breakfast and lunch the next day and force myself to go for runs or do intense workout videos at home. I constantly tell myself that I don't deserve to stop until I've "earned" back the calories I ate. Since I'm fasting at the same time, this intense physical strain leaves me feeling completely shaky, dizzy, and lightheaded.

front of the kids at all. \n- S047 (Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging): After a big night of eating, I’ll punish myself by skipping breakfast and lunch the next day and then forcing myself to jog or do workout videos until I’m shaky and lightheaded, telling myself I don’t deserve to stop until I’ve “earned” back the calories. \n- S049 (Eating_Large_Amount): When I get home after staying late to plan, I’ll tell myself I’m just going to have a quick dinner, but then I’ll end up plowing through an entire frozen pizza, a family-sized bag of chips, and whatever sweets are in the cupboard in less than an hour, eating way past the point of feeling uncomfortably full. \n- S050 (Loss_of_Control): Once I start eating like that in the evenings, it’s like a switch flips and I’m watching myself from the outside, thinking, “You need to stop,” but my hands just keep reaching for more food until everything in front of me is gone and I feel sick and ashamed. \n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- for it at least a few times every week, usually four or five nights out of seven.\n- functional_impairment: My energy and focus at school have tanked because I’m either wiped out from binging or dizzy from overexercising and skipping meals, so I’ve started calling in sick more often and avoiding staff gatherings because I’m embarrassed about how out of control I feel around food and my body.\n- Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: How I feel about my body and weight is completely controlling how I see myself and how I function at school. I'm a teacher, and lately, this obsession has just taken over my entire life to the point where I feel like I can't even do my job properly anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you currently experience any behaviors such as restricting your food intake, bingeing, or purging in an attempt to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Most nights I completely lose control and eat massive amounts of food, like a whole pizza and bags of chips, though I don't purge or throw up. Instead, I punish myself the next day by skipping breakfast and lunch and forcing myself to exercise until I'm completely lightheaded, which happens about four or five times a week.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant weight fluctuation, such as gaining or losing a substantial amount of weight, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven't noticed any major weight gain or loss, but I am constantly obsessing over the scale. If it goes up by even a single pound, I feel like a complete failure as a person and a teacher.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you don't purge or throw up after bingeing, but you do engage in excessive exercise as a form of punishment, can you tell me more about what you mean by 'forcing myself to exercise until I'm completely lightheaded'?\n\nPatient: After a huge night of eating, I'll skip breakfast and lunch the next day and force myself to go for runs or do intense workout videos at home. I carry so much deep shame after I binge, which is why I try to hide it and desperately overcompensate the next day.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how often you have these episodes of binge eating followed by excessive exercise as a means of compensation, and does this occur at least once a week for the past three months?"

Instead, I punish myself the next day by skipping breakfast and lunch and forcing myself to exercise until I'm completely lightheaded, which happens about four or five times a week.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant weight fluctuation, such as gaining or losing a substantial amount of weight, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven't noticed any major weight gain or loss, but I am constantly obsessing over the scale. If it goes up by even a single pound, I feel like a complete failure as a person and a teacher.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you don't purge or throw up after bingeing, but you do engage in excessive exercise as a form of punishment, can you tell me more about what you mean by 'forcing myself to exercise until I'm completely lightheaded'?\n\nPatient: After a huge night of eating, I'll skip breakfast and lunch the next day and force myself to go for runs or do intense workout videos at home. I constantly tell myself that I don't deserve to stop until I've \"earned\" back the calories I ate. Since I'm fasting at the same time, this intense physical strain leaves me feeling completely shaky, dizzy, and lightheaded.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your worth as a person is heavily tied to your weight and appearance, and that when you fail to meet your own expectations in this area, you feel a deep sense of guilt or shame?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. My entire self-worth is completely tied to my body and what the scale says, and if it goes up even a single pound, I feel like a complete failure as a person and a teacher. I carry so much deep shame after I binge, which is why I try to hide it and desperately overcompensate the next day.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how often you have these episodes of binge eating followed by excessive exercise as a means of compensation, and does this occur at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely happens more than once a week—usually about four or five nights out of seven. This exhausting cycle of binging and then trying to make up for it has been going on for about four months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe any other compensatory behaviors you engage in besides excessive exercise, such as fasting or using laxatives, to counteract the calories consumed during binge eating episodes?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't use laxatives, but I do fast by completely skipping breakfast and lunch the next day to punish myself. That fasting, combined with the intense jogging and workout videos, is my main way of trying to make up for the binging.

However, the patient's use of excessive exercise as a compensatory behavior, rather than traditional purging methods, and the absence of significant weight fluctuation, may suggest a need to consider other specific eating disorder diagnoses. The previous candidates of F50.2 and F50.0 are still considered, but F50.9 is added as the patient's symptoms may not fit perfectly into one specific category, and the diagnosis may require further clarification.", "is_final": false}

I feel like I am punishing myself for losing control by skipping meals and forcing myself to exercise because I don't deserve to stop, but I am also desperately trying to prevent weight gain. Since my entire self-worth is tied to the scale, these behaviors feel like the only way I can regain any sense of control."
  }

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol). Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
